"github:flashinfer-ai/flashinfer:6047", "source": "github", "kind": "pr", "url": "https://github.com/flashinfer-ai/flashinfer/pull/6047", "title": "feat(moe_ep)!: sync NVLinkOneSidedAlltoAll with TensorRT-LLM's kernels; remove the deprecated MoeAlltoAll/MnnvlMoe", "created_at": "2026-10-04T18:47:08Z", "updated_at": "2026-10-16T20:05:34Z", "fetched_at": "2026-10-20T12:16:49Z", "state": "merged", "merged_at": "2026-10-16T20:05:34Z", "parent": null, "content_hash": "77cf82ea321c185421228c11ffab5bb92684abc33027ffb02061b6d89ffc7002", "body": "<!-- .github/pull_request_template.md -->\n\n## \ud83d\udccc Description\n\nFollow-up to #5532. `flashinfer.moe_ep.NVLinkOneSidedAlltoAll` now runs its own NVLink one-sided MoE all-to-all kernels, synced with TensorRT-LLM's (`cpp/tensorrt_llm/kernels/moe/communication/` plus the later one-sided changes), instead of wrapping the deprecated `flashinfer.comm.MoeAlltoAll`. The `MoEEpCommunication` interface from #5532 is unchanged.\n\nIt also completes the deprecation started in #5532. No `MoEEpCommunication` backend builds on `MoeAlltoAll` or `MnnvlMoe` any more, so both classes are **removed** (breaking; see *Removal and migration* below). `NVLinkOneSidedAlltoAll` and `CakeAlltoAll` take over `MoeAlltoAll`'s `checkpoint_prepare` / `checkpoint_restore`.\n\n### What the new kernels bring\n\n- **CFT counted writes.** On Blackwell systems whose driver exports the CUDA logical-endpoint API, dispatch and combine can push payloads over the NVLink fabric with `fabric.try_put.counted`. The receiver then waits on hardware byte counters instead of per-round completion flags, and the CFT dispatch also fills the padding rows' expert ids itself. The logical endpoint is bound to the existing MNNVL workspace, so no extra memory is allocated.\n- **Up to 256 EP ranks** (was 64); the active-rank mask grows to four words.\n- **Workspace laid out by phase.** Round state, dispatch control and payload, and combine control, input and the CFT receive inbox are separate regions with fixed capacities, which every op validates.\n- **Routing and fan-out.**\n  - Invalid expert ids are skipped during routing.\n  - When `top_k > ep_size`, dispatch and combine fan out over a compact per-rank destination list.\n  - Receive counters are double-buffered by round parity; the CFT dispatch publishes them right after routing.\n- **Low-precision combine** pairs FP8 conversions.\n- **Timeouts** are chosen on the host per launch.\n\n### Python\n\n- `NVLinkOneSidedAlltoAll` drives the new JIT module (`moe_ep_nvlink_one_sided_alltoall`) directly.\n  - **Workspace sharing:** instances with the same layout share one workspace, which is freed when its last instance is destroyed. Instances on one workspace must run their rounds one after another; a dispatch while another instance's round awaits its combine raises.\n  - **CFT setup:** endpoint creation and exchange run over the EP group's `CommBackend`, so no MPI is involved. Every rank agrees on CFT before the layout is fixed, and a failure on any rank falls back to the fence path on all ranks.\n- `NVLinkOneSidedConfig` gains:\n  - `cft`: `None` uses CFT automatically where supported, for steps up to `cft_max_tokens_for_dispatch` / `cft_max_tokens_for_combine` tokens (128 each); `True` uses it on every supported step; `False` never uses it;\n  - `timeout_sec`: the bound on each wait for a peer;\n  - `enable_pdl`: programmatic dependent launch, `None` enabling it on SM90+ as before (also on `CakeAlltoAllConfig`).\n- `NVLinkOneSidedAlltoAll.active_rank_mask(ranks)` builds the 256-rank mask, and `cft_enabled` reports whether CFT is available to the instance.\n- **`CakeAlltoAll`** is now a standalone `MoEEpCommunication` (it used to subclass `NVLinkOneSidedAlltoAll`). The generated Cake kernels implement the protocol of the functional `moe_a2a_*` ops, which it calls with `backend=\"cake\"`; those ops are unchanged. `CakeAlltoAll` pins the combine region at the end of the workspace instead of using the offset each dispatch returns, which moves with the token count (the race #6376 fixed in `MoeAlltoAll`), and follows the same one-round-per-workspace rule.\n- `benchmarks/moe_ep/core/comm/bench_moe_ep_comm.py` gets `--cft {auto,on,off}`.\n- The module is in the AOT build list for SM90+.\n\n### Checkpointing\n\n`NVLinkOneSidedAlltoAll` and `CakeAlltoAll` gain `checkpoint_prepare()` and `checkpoint_restore(comm_backend)`, with the semantics `MoeAlltoAll` had (callers such as vLLM used to reach them through the former `.alltoall` accessor):\n\n- **Prepare:** releases the physical MNNVL memory and keeps the virtual addresses reserved.\n- **Restore:** remaps the memory over a fresh `CommBackend` and re-initializes the workspace. CUDA graphs captured before the checkpoint replay correctly afterwards.\n- **Shared rules:** both calls are collective, and repeated calls do nothing.\n- **CFT endpoints:**\n  - `NVLinkOneSidedAlltoAll` also releases its CFT logical endpoints before the memory and recreates them afterwards.\n  - Launches bake the endpoint IDs into their kernel parameters, so the endpoints keep their ID block reserved across the checkpoint (`moe_a2a_cft_release_endpoints`) and are recreated under the same IDs.\n  - Restore verifies the IDs and raises if they changed.\n- **CftLeManager fix:** it now tracks imported peer endpoints explicitly. It used to treat ID 0 as unset, and ID blocks start at 0, so the import of rank 0's endpoint was never destroyed. The kernels' copy in TensorRT-LLM has the same bug.\n- The shared MNNVL helpers are `unmap_mnnvl_memory` / `remap_mnnvl_", "body_excerpted": true, "metadata_excerpt": "{\"number\": 6047, \"labels\": [{\"id\": 10040634866, \"node_id\": \"LA_kwDOJ-UyCM8AAAACVnft8g\", \"url\": \"https://api.github.com/repos/flashinfer-ai/flashinfer/labels/run-ci\", \"name\": \"run-ci\", \"color\": \"D7BD0D\", \"default\": false, \"description\": \"\", \"archived_at\": null, \"archived_by\": null}, {\"id\": 10086440248, \"node_id\": \"LA_kwDOJ-UyCM8AAAACWTLdOA\", \"url\": \"https://api.github.com/repos/flashinfer-ai/flashinfer/labels/op:%20comm\", \"name\": \"op: comm\", \"color\": \"f3e577\", \"default\": false, \"description\": \"\", \"archived_at\": null, \"archived_by\": null}], \"comments\": 7, \"state_reason\": null}", "metadata_excerpted": false}, {"id": "github:flashinfer-ai/flashinfer:6587:live-page", "source": "github", "kind": "resource", "url": "https://github.com/flashinfer-ai/flashinfer/issues/6587", "title": "[Feature]: Deepseek V4.1 Flash support on CAKE for TP8 and TP4 \u00b7 Issue #6587 \u00b7 flashinfer-ai/flashinfer \u00b7 GitHub", "created_at": null, "updated_at": null, "fetched_at": "2026-10-20T12:16:04Z", "state": null, "merged_at": null, "parent": "github:flashinfer-ai/flashinfer:6587", "content_hash": "237c5aadca06ffb76e58d3a2e1cc8a4582e9a4fbb5e2032f8d23216ae2744022", "body": "[Feature]: Deepseek V4.1 Flash support on CAKE for TP8 and TP4\n#6587\nNew issue\nCopy link\nNew issue\nCopy link\nOpen\nOpen\n[Feature]: Deepseek V4.1 Flash support on CAKE for TP8 and TP4\n#6587\nCopy link\nLabels\nfeature request\nNew feature or request\nNew feature or request\nneeds-triage\nDescription\nyikaizhu-baseten\nopened\non Oct 16, 2026\nIssue body actions\nBefore submitting\nI have searched existing issues and this request has not been filed yet.\nProblem\nWhen i run benchmark, it seems for TP4 and TP8, CAKE doesn't support deepseek v4.1 Flash shapes. Beam support hasn't shipped in any vLLM release or nightly yet, so the recipe is Docker-only on the `vllm/vllm-openai:reflection-beam` image.\n\n### What the builder renders\n\n| Hardware | Default command | Status |\n|---|---|---|\n| B300 (page default) | NVFP4, TP4, `--attention-backend FLASHINFER --moe-backend flashinfer_trtllm` | \u2705 verified, release checkpoint |\n| B200 / GB200 / GB300 | NVFP4, TP4 (same flags) | eligible, not run |\n| H200 | NVFP4 via Marlin, TP8. **Recommended: the FP8 variant, TP8** | \u2705 verified (FP8 and NVFP4), proto checkpoints |\n| H100 | NVFP4 via Marlin, TP8 | 80 GB budget simulated on H200 |\n\n- Variants: `default` = NVFP4 (`reflection/Beam-1.0-501B-A23B-NVFP4`), `fp8` (`-FP8`), `bf16` (base repo, only on 8x B200/B300).\n- The site has no per-GPU default variant, so H200 also opens on NVFP4. No pip path: the nightly wheels don't carry the Beam implementation.\n- Hardware: NVIDIA only. The four AMD profiles are marked `unsupported` (CUDA-only kernels, CUDA-only image).\n- `date_added`: 2026-10-16, the launch day.\n\n### Evidence\n\nEach verified run passed: `/health`, `/v1/models`, chat with `finish_reason: stop`, `reasoning_content`, parsed `tool_calls`.\n\nThe numbers below stay out of the recipe, following the repo convention: few recipes publish measured throughput, and upstream held back such a PR as premature (#58). Workload: 8 chat prompts (code, math, prose, translation) \u00d7 8 runs, up to 1,500 output tokens, temperature 0.6, reasoning on. Every command is the one the site generates. Two independent causes; both are needed on current main.\n\n**1. Draft rows mirrored before the drafter writes them.** The MTP layers are HiSparse layers too, and HiSparse marks a page clean and releases it for all of its layers, target and draft, at once. Each step does:\n\n```\nstart_step \u2192 target forward \u2192 finish_forward \u2192 propose (drafter writes its rows)\n```\n\n`finish_forward` mirrored *all* layers to host, including draft layers whose rows `propose` had not written yet, and then reported the step's pages clean. The drafter's own per-layer mirror is skipped under FULL graphs, so the fresh draft rows never reached host. When the page's GPU block was reused, the drafter read stale host rows.\n\n**2. The residency resolver can read stale request ids** (regression from #59235). The resolver runs on the HiSparse copy stream, which outside PIECEWISE graphs waits only on `logical_topk_ready`, recorded right after the indexer. #59235 writes the masked request ids later, on the compute stream, so the resolver can read the previous call's ids. The MTP layer alternates four rows per request (draft prefill) with one (draft decode), so stale ids resolve draft rows against other requests' KV.\n\n## Changes\n\n- The speculator tags its draft attention layers (`AttentionLayerBase.is_draft_layer`); HiSparse binding marks their cache handles as draft layers.\n- `finish_forward` mirrors only target layers.\n- The next step's `start_step` first mirrors the previous step's draft-layer rows (the drafter has run by then), then submits that step's page transfers behind them on the same DMA stream, so a page is only reported clean after its draft rows are queued. They are queued before the step swaps host write events, so the new step's forward and host copies wait for them: by then the scheduler may have handed a finished request's GPU and host blocks to another request. No connector API changes (`wait_for_save` stays a no-op).\n- Build the resolver's masked request ids on the copy stream after its wait, into buffers owned by the HiSparse index group (moved from the generic `SparseMLAIndexGroup`); `swap_in` now requires the valid-row count.\n\n**Why not just move `finish_forward` after `propose`?** Outside FULL graphs the drafter re-prepares the draft handles with its own (decode-shaped) metadata, so a combined end-of-step mirror hits HiSparse's metadata consistency checks (`RuntimeError`) for every target/drafter graph-mode mix except FULL/FULL. Target and draft layers need to be mirrored at different points, which requires knowing which layers are draft layers.\n\n**Known limitation (follow-up).** A host block freed after step N (finished or preempted request) can be reallocated to a P/D import before step N's deferred copies land; NIXL writes are not ordered against the copy stream, so an imported request could, rarely, receive stale draft-layer rows (or, with the non-default lazy mirror mode, stale rows for all layers of a preempted request's spilled page). GPU writes and host copy-on-write are ordered: the next step waits for the deferred copies before its forward and host copies. The follow-up holds freed host blocks until the step's deferred writes are reported done.\n\n## Test Plan / Results\n\n**Unit:**\n- `test_draft_layer_rows_mirrored_after_drafter[FULL|NONE]` drives the real worker and checks that host draft rows equal the drafter's writes, with exactly one draft-layer copy and the page reported only after it. On main the FULL case fails.\n- `test_hisparse_resolver_reads_current_request_ids` (GPU) stalls the compute stream between `logical_topk_ready` and attention, then resolves four rows per request followed by one row per request plus a padding row. It fails on main and passes here.\n- H100: `test_sparse_mla_backends.py -k \"hisparse or index_group\"` 40 passed; HiSparse connector/worker tests pass.\n- `tests/v1/e2e/general/test_hisparse.py`: the FULL-graph host-mirror check now verifies MTP rows when the next step's start mirrors them (it compared them right after `finish_forward`, before the drafter writes them) and requires the MTP layer to be detected as a draft layer. 4 passed (H100); fails if the speculator stops tagging its layers.\n\n**E2E, cause 1 (on the pre-#59235 base):** H200 2\u00d78, GLM-5.3 TP8/EP8 HiSparse decoder, MTP3, FULL_AND_PIECEWISE, NIXL P/D from a PCP8/DCP8 prefiller; Agent X workload at concurrency 48 (HiSparse GPU pool \u226595%).\n\n| | MTP acceptance | per position | output tok/s | ITL p50 |\n| --- | --- 0.471 | 0.66/0.45/0.30 | 664.6 | 51.8 ms |\n\n\\* includes debug-counter overhead; the pre-fix sweep peaked at ~500 tok/s.\n\n- Host-vs-GPU audit of sealed MTP-layer pages: 98.7% mismatch before, 6", "168e4355bc84640d3feeca7f230dee8cb56e11c4ff02ad74fd8bc795b52124f6", "body": "## Summary\r\n\r\nPR adaptive DSpark verification for MiniMax M3 on ROCm. Part of the adaptive DSpark backend expansion (#51303): each model needs varlen-capable sparse kernels before adaptive verification can redistribute draft tokens across requests\r\nIt was validated end to end with amd/MiniMax-M3-MXFP4 and nvidia/MiniMax-M3-DSpark on MI325.\r\n\r\nScope: only the ROCm Triton indexer and sparse-decode paths gain ragged decode support. The CUDA and AITER MiniMax M3 backends still report device/CPU query-length mismatch as unsupported, so adaptive verification stays disabled there. With adaptive verification off, the uniform decode path is unchanged.\r\n\r\n## Changes\r\n\r\n- MiniMax M3 indexer and sparse attention (ROCm Triton): device-side ragged decode metadata; both AMD index-score launch policies, the fused top-k selector, and sparse decode map packed query tokens to requests from device `query_start_loc`.\r\n- `kernel_paged_attention_2d`: return for `query_len != 1` (previously `> 1`). FULL cudagraph replay can pad the request dimension with zero-length rows, which fell into the decode path and read one row past the logical query buffer.\r\n- Rejection sampler: adaptive verification allows the regular chunked path for batches with no draft tokens, whose logits layout isnever compacted.\r\n- Warmup: synthetic draft rows are bounded by the same rejection-sampler budget used at runtime, so large `max_num_seqs` configs nolonger fail at startup.\r\n- Adaptive verification overhead: confidence D2H copies use a non-blocking ring, and only TP rank 0 computes the draft budget, thenbroadcasts it.\r\n- DSpark config: prefer speculators-format top-level confidence-head fields and fall back to NVIDIA's legacy `dflash_config` fields.\r\n\r\n\r\nToken-to-request mapping uses upstream's `compute_token_to_req_indices`, a device-side binary search over `query_start_loc`. It doesn't depend on `max_query_len` and handles zero-length rows and the padding tail.\r\n\r\n## Not a duplicate\r\n\r\nSearched open PRs (adaptive verification, MiniMax M3 DSpark, token-to-request mapping, paged-decode zero-length). The closest is #52893, which skips AITER sparse PA under spec decode; this PR adds ragged decode to the Triton path instead.\r\n\r\n## Testing\r\n\r\nOn MI325:\r\n- `tests/v1/spec_decode/test_adaptive_verification.py`, `tests/v1/worker/test_gpu_rejection_sampler_chunking.py`, `tests/v1/worker/test_gpu_warmup_blocks.py`, `tests/v1/worker/test_attn_utils.py`: 138 passed, 1 skipped.\r\n- `tests/kernels/attention/test_minimax_m3.py`: all passed.\r\n- `test_prefix_prefill.py::test_contexted_kv_attention` (`chunked_prefill_paged_decode`, source build of this branch): 128 passed, 256 skipped.\r\n\r\n- Stress test of the ragged index top-k and sparse decode kernels at serving-sized batches: 60 random batches of 380\u2013512 requests (1\u20138 query tokens each, up to 4K context, zero-length padding rows), reference-checked every 10th batch: all passed.\r\n\r\n## End-to-end results \r\n\r\nMI325, `nvidia/MiniMax-M3-DSpark` drafter with its default confidence head, `num_speculative_tokens=7`, FULL cudagraphs, default memory settings, `--max-model-len 4096 --max-num-seqs 512`.
